        `c3c4` moves the King to `c4`.
        `c3d2` moves the King to `d2`.
        `c3b2` moves the King to `b2`.

        Let's evaluate these moves:
        - `c3d4`: Moves away from the Queen's check on the 3rd rank. It moves to a central square.
        - `c3c4`: Moves away from check, but maybe into the attack of the pawn at `b3` (if `c4` is attacked).
        - `c3d2`: Moves away from check. Moves closer to the Bishop at `d1`.
        - `c3b2`: Moves away from check.

        Given the position, White is lost. The priority is to move the King to a square where it's not immediately captured or put in check again.
        If the Queen is at `g3`:
        - `d4` is not on the 3rd rank, not on the g-file, not on the g3-d4 diagonal. (g3 to d4: delta-x=3, delta-y=1. Not a diagonal).
        - `c4` is not on the 3rd rank, not on the g-file, not on the g3-c4 diagonal (g3 to c4: delta-x=4, delta-y=1. Not a diagonal).
        - `d2` is not on the 3rd rank, not on the g-file, not on the g3-d2 diagonal (g3 to d2: delta-x=3, delta-y=1. Not a diagonal).
        - `b2` is not on the 3rd rank, not on the g-file, not on the g3-b2 diagonal (g3 to b2: delta-x=5, delta-y=1. Not a diagonal).

        Now let's check the Bishop at `d1`.
        `d1` is a light square.
        Squares:
        - `d4` is a dark square. (Safe from Bishop `d1`).
        - `c4` is a dark square. (Safe from Bishop `d1`).
        - `d2` is a light square. (ATTACKED by Bishop `d1`!).
        - `b2` is a light square. (ATTACKED by Bishop `d1`!).
        Wait, if `d1` is the Bishop, it attacks `b3, c2, e2, f3...` and `d1-a4` diagonal.
        Let's check `d1` attacks:
        Diagonal 1: `d1, e2, f3, g4, h5`.
        Diagonal 2: `d1, c2, b3, a4`.
        So `d2` and `b2` are not on the diagonals from `d1`.
        Wait, the Bishop moves diagonally. `d1` is a light square. `d2` is a dark square. `b2` is a dark square.
        So Bishop at `d1` cannot attack `d2` or `b2`.

        Let's re-verify:
        `d1` (light) -> `c2` (dark), `e2` (dark). NO.
        `d1` (light) -> `e2` is light? No.
        Coordinates:
        a1=dark, b1=light, c1=dark, d1=light, e1=dark, f1=light, g1=dark, h1=light.
        a2=light, b2=dark, c2=light, d2=dark, e2=light, f2=dark, g2=light, h2=dark.
        So:
        - `d1` is light.
        - `d2` is dark.
        - `b2` is dark.
        - `c4` is dark.
        - `d4` is light.

        Bishop `d1` (light) attacks light squares: `e2, f3, g4, h5` and `c2, b3, a4`.
        So:
        - `d4` (light) is attacked by Bishop `d1`? Let's see. `d1-e2-f3-g4`. No. `d1-c2-b3-a4`. No.
        Actually, the diagonal is `d1, e2, f3, g4, h5`. `d4` is not on this.
        Wait, the other diagonal is `d1, c2, b3, a4`. `d4` is not on this either.
        So `d4` is safe from the Bishop.

        Let's check the Queen at `g3` again.
        Queen `g3` (light square).
        `g3` attacks everything on rank 3, file g, and diagonals.
        Diagonals from `g3`:
        - `g3-f4-e5-d6-c7-b8`
        - `g3-h4`
        - `g3-f2-e1`
        - `g3-h2`
        So `d4, c4, d2, b2` are all safe from the Queen.

        Now let's check the pawn at `b3`.
        Pawn `b3` (black) attacks `a4` and `c4`.
        So `c3c4` is attacked by the pawn!
        If the pawn is at `b3`, `c4` is attacked.
        Then why is `c3c4` listed as a legal move?
        Maybe the pawn is at `b4`?
        If the pawn is at `b4`, it attacks `a3` and `c3`.
        But the King is at `c3`. That would mean the King is under attack by the pawn.
        If the pawn is at `b4`, then `c4` is not attacked.
